9.4. Closing the bootstrap and fixing every negative-time measure.
Proposition 9.5 (Fixed-frame convergence and uniqueness). The rescaled flow converges
smoothly on every fixed compact set, at all rescaled times, to S in the original ambient
coordinates. For the smooth closed embedded surface flow and fixed first singular point of
Theorem 1.1, √every backward tangent flow consequently equals the multiplicity-one homo-
thetic motion −s S at every negative time s.
Proof. Suppose first that the weak core bootstrap has a finite first stopping time L. Use
only intervals [i, i + 1] whose right endpoints lie strictly before L. If i is the last such index,
then
                    i + 1 < L ≤ i + 2,     τi ≥ i + 0.2,      L − τi ≤ 1.8 < 2.              (9.20)
Allowing a completed interval ending at L by continuity only shortens the gap. If no
such interval is available, the initial tangent-convergence window supplies the graphs and
derivatives through this prospective stopping time.
   For a last available slice, propagate directly from its improved graph using Lemma 9.2
over the fixed horizon U = 1 − e−2 < 1. No subsequent selected slice, localized inequality, √
or future unit energy drop is needed. Even a limiting cylinder retains radius at least 2e−1
on this horizon, so all patch constants and buffers were fixed with a genuine pre-extinction
margin. Choose the minimum graphical radius so large that this finite additive buffer leaves
the fixed bootstrap ball and its surrounding collar strictly inside the propagated domain.
This gives graphical coverage there through L.
   Uniform derivatives near the last selected time require a preceding positive delay. If
i ≥ 1, use the propagation from τi−1 on
                                       [τi , min{L, τi + 0.2}],
where the rescaled delay lies in [0.8, 1.4], and use the propagation from τi on [τi + 0.2, L],
where it lies in [0.2, 1.8]. Both propagations are available on their entire two-unit horizons
from the already proved improved graphs; the earlier induction’s use of part of a horizon
does not shorten it. On every completed selected interval [τj , τj+1 ] with j ≥ 1, use τj−1
for the first 0.2 time units and τj thereafter. The initial two-unit tangent window supplies
the first entrance and the cases i = 0 or no complete interval. In these covers the ordinary
elapsed times are bounded below by 1−e−0.2 , and the dilation factors are uniformly bounded.
Lemma 9.2 therefore supplies uniform interior curvature derivatives on the fixed ball and
collar. The weak fixed-core graph and the bounded geometry of S convert these geometric
estimates to uniform derivatives of its normal height, as needed for interpolation below.
This uses only known selected slices and no future energy drop. The complete graphical
coverage also excludes an entering extra sheet or a loss at the boundary of the observation
domain at the stopping time.
98                                                OPENAI


  The length through completed intervals is bounded by Lemma 9.4. The entire remaining
segment from the last selected slice costs at most
                                                                  !1/2
                     ∫ L                √       ∫ L                          √
                           d(τ ) dτ ≤       2         d(τ )2 dτ          ≤       2E(0)1/2 .   (9.21)
                      τi                         τi

Counting part of a complete interval twice only weakens this upper bound. The initial-
window case has the same estimate with its bounded duration. The graph-speed formula
used in (9.15) therefore bounds the change of the fixed-core graph in L2 by
                                    C E(0)p + B(Rin ) + E(0)1/2 .
                                                                         

The initial graph can be chosen much closer to S than the bootstrap tolerance. Interpolation
with the uniform interior derivatives on the larger collar makes the resulting C 1 distance
strictly smaller than that tolerance through L. The already established coverage and these
strict estimates extend the bootstrap past L, a contradiction. This proves that the bootstrap
and the radius induction hold for all time.
   Letting the prefix tend to infinity in (9.17) gives
                               ∫ ∞                            ∑
                                      d(τ ) dτ < ∞,                 aj < ∞.                   (9.22)
                                0                             j≥0

On the fixed observation core the graph functions are Cauchy in L2 , and the uniform
derivative bounds make them converge smoothly on a slightly smaller core. Their limit
is S, because the original tangent sequence still has a subsequence at arbitrarily large
rescaled times and converges there to S. In particular (9.15) gives (bj , xj ) → (0, 0) in the
fixed compact observation chart.
   The radii actually diverge. Since ai → 0, their logarithmic cutoffs ri tend to infinity.
Given H < ∞, choose I such that ri ≥ H for i ≥ I. Every recent term Qj−i ri , I ≤ i ≤ j,
is then at least H; each of the finitely many earlier terms and Qj Rin eventually exceeds H.
Taking the minimum in (9.11) proves Rj → ∞.
   The improved graphs at τj thus have height and slope tending to zero on every fixed
compact subset of Sbj . Their base compact multipliers tend to zero by (8.8). Apply the
error-modulus version of Lemma 9.2 on every interval [τj , τj+1 ]. In the rescaled coordinates
its homothetic comparison is exactly the fixed set Sbj , because the comparison shrinkage
is canceled by the rescaling. Since bj → 0, the actual graphs converge to S at every
intermediate time, uniformly on each compact spatial set. The higher derivative upgrade
always uses a preceding slice with positive delay. For τ ∈ [τj , τj+1 ], use the slice τj if
τ − τj ≥ 0.2, giving rescaled delay in [0.2, 1.2]. If 0 ≤ τ − τj < 0.2, use τj−1 , giving delay
in [0.8, 1.4]. Both delays are bounded below by 0.2 and above by the fixed horizon 2, and
therefore their ordinary elapsed times are bounded below by 1 − e−0.2 > 0 and above by
1−e−2 < 1. The dilation factors are uniformly bounded. Since the preceding graphical radii
tend to infinity, every fixed compact set and its interior margin are eventually contained in
these propagated domains. Equation (9.7) supplies uniform bounds in every fixed derivative
order there; interpolation with the established C 1 convergence gives smooth convergence,
including at each selected endpoint. No curvature estimate at zero elapsed time is used.
Thus convergence holds in the original ambient frame at every rescaled time.
   Restore the original, unshifted rescaled clock. For every s < 0 and every admissible λ
there is the exact identity
                                     √
                               Msλ = −s M 2 log λ − log(−s) .
                                                               
                                                                                         (9.23)
The rescaled times on the right tend to infinity, uniformly for s in any compact interval
[−B, −b] ⊂ (−∞, 0). Consequently the full family
                                              √ of actual surfaces on the left converges
locally smoothly and with multiplicity one to −s S. Smooth complete graphical conver-
gence gives both local area-measure and varifold convergence for every individual s < 0.
                                  UNIQUENESS OF TANGENT FLOWS                                           99


The identity uses only the original fixed center and scalar dilation, so it preserves the axes
and all ambient positions. This proves the asserted smooth convergence. To pass from con-
vergence of the actual slices to every slice of a weak representative, we use the all-endpoint
convention and the weak-limit premise specified below.                                      □
Completion of the proof of Proposition 9.5. Here is the precise weak-limit premise sufficient
for the representative assertion. Write µλs = H2⌞Msλ . A tangent Radon family (µs )s<0 is
assumed to satisfy the local hypotheses and the all-endpoint inequality of Lemma 2.1, and
along its extracted scales λi → ∞ to satisfy
    ∫∫                               ∫∫
         ψ(X, s) dµλs i (X) ds −→                                  ψ ∈ Cc (R3 × (−∞, 0)) .
                                                                                             
                                          ψ(X, s) dµs (X) ds                                        (9.24)
For each test, the left side is considered once its time support lies in the interval on which
the rescaled flow is defined. The local mass condition in the lemma makes the right-hand
spacetime weight a locally finite Radon measure. This is the only weak-limit identification
used here. Local weak convergence µλs i ⇀ µs for almost every s, together with a uniform
bound for µλs i (BR ) on each compact time interval and for each fixed R, also implies (9.24)
by dominated convergence.
   The
   ∫∫ direct smooth convergence √ already proved shows that the left side of (9.24) converges
to    ψ dνs ds, with νs = H2⌞( −s S). To justify the time integration, place the support of
ψ in BR × J with J ⋐ (−∞, 0). The scale-invariant area-ratio bound gives µλs i (BR ) ≤ ΛR2 ,
so dominated convergence applies to the slice integrals. Uniqueness of the local weak Radon
limit therefore gives µs ds = νs ds as spacetime measures.
   Choose a countable family of compactly supported smooth spatial tests that determines
Radon measures, for example a family dense on each ball in the uniform norm. Test this
spacetime equality by the product of each spatial test and an arbitrary compactly supported
continuous time test. Local integrability implies equality of the corresponding slice integrals
almost everywhere for each spatial test. Intersecting these countably many full-measure sets
and using the determining property yields µs = νs as Radon measures for almost every s.
Lemma 2.1 now gives this equality for every s < 0. The argument applies to every all-
endpoint representative satisfying (9.24), so no choice of its exceptional slices can change
the negative-time measures. This proves the representative conclusion under the stated
convention.                                                                                  □
Proof of Theorem 1.1. Fix a singular point at the first singular time. The preceding con-
struction applies to its tangent section from Theorem 2.2, and Proposition 9.5 gives the full
fixed-frame smooth convergence and the equality of every negative-time measure under the
stated tangent convention. The point was arbitrary.                                        □
